# Volt Documentation

[Crypt](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt)

crypt.generatebytes
===================

Generates cryptographically secure random bytes.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes#syntax)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    crypt.generatebytes(length: number) -> string

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes#parameters)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `length` | `number` | Number of bytes to generate |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes#returns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | Base64-encoded random bytes |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes#description)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`crypt.generatebytes` generates `length` cryptographically secure random bytes and returns their Base64 representation. `length` must be between 0 and 512.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes#example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Generate 16 bytes for an IV
    local iv = crypt.generatebytes(16)
    assert(#crypt.base64decode(iv) == 16)
    
    -- Generate 32 bytes for a salt
    local salt = crypt.generatebytes(32)
    assert(#crypt.base64decode(salt) == 32)

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes#related-functions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`crypt.random`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/random)
     - Generate raw random bytes
*   [`crypt.generatekey`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey)
     - Generate encryption key

rconsoletoggle
==============

Toggles the console window visibility.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletoggle#syntax)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoletoggle() -> ()

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletoggle#returns)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletoggle#description)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsoletoggle` toggles the visibility of the console window. If hidden, it shows the console; if visible, it hides the console.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletoggle#example)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Create console first
    rconsoleshow()
    rconsoleprint("Console is visible\n")
    
    -- Toggle it off
    rconsoletoggle()
    print("Console is now hidden")
    
    -- Toggle it back on
    rconsoletoggle()
    rconsoleprint("Console is visible again\n")

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletoggle#related-functions)

# Volt Documentation

[Console](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console)

rconsoleprint
=============

Prints text to the console window.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#syntax)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleprint(data: any, async?: boolean, escape?: boolean) -> ()

[Aliases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#aliases)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `consoleprint`

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#parameters)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `data` | `any` | Value to convert with `tostring` and print |
| `async` | `boolean?` | Yield while writing on the console task (default: true) |
| `escape` | `boolean?` | Preserve the full string length, including embedded nulls (default: true) |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#returns)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#description)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsoleprint` outputs text to the console window. Unlike the other console print functions, this prints plain text without any formatting or prefix.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#example)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow()
    
    rconsoleprint("Hello, World!")
    rconsoleprint({ answer = 42 }, false)

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#notes)

------------------------------------------------------------------

*   A newline is appended automatically
*   Tokens such as `@@RED@@` change the console color instead of printing text

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint#related-functions)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`rconsoleerr`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleerr)

rconsoletop
===========

Sets whether the console window stays above other windows.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletop#syntax)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoletop(shouldBeTop: boolean) -> ()

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletop#returns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletop#description)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsoletop` makes the console topmost when passed `true` and removes that state when passed `false`. It also shows the console.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletop#example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow()
    rconsoleprint("This console is now on top!\n")
    
    -- Bring console to front
    rconsoletop(true)

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletop#notes)

----------------------------------------------------------------

*   Call `rconsoletop(false)` to restore normal window ordering

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoletop#related-functions)

rconsoleshow
============

Creates and shows the console window.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow() -> ()

[Aliases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#aliases)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `rconsolecreate`
*   `consolecreate`

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsoleshow` creates a new console window if one doesn't exist, and shows it. The console can be used to display output and receive user input.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Create and show the console
    rconsoleshow()
    
    -- Print a welcome message
    rconsoleprint("Welcome to the console!\n")
    
    -- Set a custom title
    rconsolename("My Script Console")

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#notes)

-----------------------------------------------------------------

*   Only one console window can exist at a time
*   The console persists until `rconsolehide` is called or the game closes
*   Use `\n` for newlines in console output

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleshow#related-functions)

rconsolehidden
==============

Checks if the console window is hidden.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsolehidden#syntax)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsolehidden() -> boolean

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `boolean` | True if console is hidden/closed |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsolehidden#description)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsolehidden` returns whether the console window is currently hidden or not visible.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsolehidden#example)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow()
    
    if not rconsolehidden() then
        rconsoleprint("Console is visible!\n")
    end
    
    rconsolehide()
    
    if rconsolehidden() then
        print("Console is now hidden")
    end

# Volt Documentation

[Input](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input)

keypress
========

Simulates pressing a keyboard key.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------

    keypress(keycode: number) -> ()

[Aliases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#aliases)

---------------------------------------------------------------

*   `keytap`

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#parameters)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `keycode` | `number` | Windows virtual-key code to press |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

`keypress` simulates pressing a keyboard key down. The key stays pressed until `keyrelease` is called.

[Common Key Codes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#common-key-codes)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Key | Code |
| --- | --- |
| Space | `0x20` |
| Enter | `0x0D` |
| Escape | `0x1B` |
| Shift | `0x10` |
| Control | `0x11` |
| A-Z | `0x41-0x5A` |
| 0-9 | `0x30-0x39` |

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Press and release the W key
    keypress(0x57)
    task.wait(0.5)
    keyrelease(0x57)

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#notes)

-----------------------------------------------------------

*   Use Windows virtual-key codes
*   Always release keys that you press
*   Check `iswindowactive()` before simulating input

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress#related-functions)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`keyrelease`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keyrelease)
     - Release a key
*   [`keyclick`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keyclick)
     - Press and release

# Volt Documentation

[Input](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input)

iswindowactive
==============

Checks if the game window is currently focused.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    iswindowactive() -> boolean

[Aliases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#aliases)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `isrbxactive`
*   `isgameactive`

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `boolean` | True if game window is focused |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`iswindowactive` returns whether the game window is currently active and focused. This is useful for ensuring simulated input occurs only while the game is focused.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Only simulate input when game is focused
    if iswindowactive() then
        keypress(0x20) -- Press space
        task.wait(0.1)
        keyrelease(0x20)
    end

[Wait for Focus](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#wait-for-focus)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Wait until game is focused before executing
    repeat task.wait() until iswindowactive()
    print("Game is now focused!")

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/iswindowactive#related-functions)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`keypress`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/keypress)
     - Simulate key press
*   [`mouse1click`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mouse1click)
     - Simulate mouse click

---------------------------------------------------------------------

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsolehide` hides the existing console window. Its contents remain available when the console is shown again.

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsolehide()

    end

# Volt Documentation

[WebSocket](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket)

WebSocket:Send
==============

Sends a message through the WebSocket connection.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------

    WebSocket:Send(message: string, isBinary?: boolean) -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#parameters)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `message` | `string` | The message to send |
| `isBinary` | `boolean?` | Send a binary frame when true; defaults to false |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------

This method does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

`WebSocket:Send` transmits the bytes in `message` as a text frame by default, or as a binary frame when `isBinary` is true. Calling it after `Close` is a no-op.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    -- Send a simple message
    ws:Send("Hello, Server!")

[JSON Communication](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#json-communication)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local HttpService = game:GetService("HttpService")
    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    -- Send JSON data
    local function sendJSON(data)
        ws:Send(HttpService:JSONEncode(data))
    end
    
    sendJSON({
        type = "subscribe",
        channel = "updates"
    })
    
    sendJSON({
        type = "message",
        content = "Hello!",
        timestamp = os.time()
    })

[Binary Message](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#binary-message)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    local bytes = string.char(0x00, 0x01, 0x02, 0xFF)
    ws:Send(bytes, true)

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#notes)

-----------------------------------------------------------

*   Luau strings can contain arbitrary bytes
*   `Send` does not return a delivery acknowledgement
*   Check `IsClosed` before sending if another part of your script may close the client

[Related](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send#related)

---------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`WebSocket.connect`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect)
     - Create connection
*   [`OnMessage`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage)
     - Receive responses

[WebSocket.OnMessage\
\
Previous Page](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage)
[RakNet\
\
Next Page](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/raknet)

# Volt Documentation

[Filesystem](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem)

writefile
=========

Writes data to a file.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    writefile(path: string, data: string) -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#parameters)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path` | `string` | The file path |
| `data` | `string` | The data to write |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`writefile` writes data to a file, creating the file if it doesn't exist or overwriting it if it does. Parent folders are created automatically.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Write a simple text file
    writefile("hello.txt", "Hello, World!")
    
    -- Verify the content
    print(readfile("hello.txt")) -- "Hello, World!"

[Writing JSON](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#writing-json)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local HttpService = game:GetService("HttpService")
    
    local settings = {
        volume = 0.5,
        graphics = "high",
        keybinds = {
            toggle = "F1",
            menu = "F2"
        }
    }
    
    writefile("settings.json", HttpService:JSONEncode(settings))

[Writing to Subfolders](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#writing-to-subfolders)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Parent folders are created automatically
    writefile("data/saves/slot1.txt", "Save data here")
    
    -- Or create them explicitly
    makefolder("logs")
    writefile("logs/session.log", "Session started")

[Binary Data](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#binary-data)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Write binary data (as string)
    local binary = string.char(0, 1, 2, 3, 255)
    writefile("binary.bin", binary)

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/writefile#related-functions)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

     - Append to a file
*   [`isfile`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/isfile)
     - Check if file exists

# Volt Release Notes

Volt 1.3
========

Volt 1.3 adds UI 1.0, Account Manager, Game Explorer, Script Viewer, Save Instance, and the Luau decompiler.

Volt 1.3 adds tools for inspecting games, viewing and decompiling scripts, saving instances, and managing account launches. It also includes executor API updates and UI 1.0.

| Component | Version |
| --- | --- |
| Module | `1.3.0.0` |
| UI  | `1.0.0` |

[Executor](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#executor)

---------------------------------------------------------------

This release fixes various executor bugs and introduces new behavior across the runtime and API. Luau LSP types were also updated.

### [Runtime and API updates](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#runtime-and-api-updates)

*   Fixed issues affecting [`hookfunction`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/hookfunction)
    , [`oth.hook`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/oth/hook)
    , RakNet packet hooks, auto-execution, workspace folder creation, and keyboard paste input.
*   Fixed `setfenv` interactions with [`setstackhidden`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/setstackhidden)
     and `oth` hooks.
*   [`gethiddenproperty`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/gethiddenproperty)
    , [`gethiddenproperties`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/gethiddenproperties)
    , [`sethiddenproperty`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/sethiddenproperty)
    , [`getproperties`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/getproperties)
    , [`isscriptable`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable)
    , [`setscriptable`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable)
    , and [`getcallbackvalue`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/instances/getcallbackvalue)
     now accept `Object` instead of only `Instance`.
*   [`getconnections`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/signals/getconnections)
     now works with non-scriptable signals.
*   [`getscriptbytecode`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/scripts/getscriptbytecode)
    , [`getscriptclosure`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/scripts/getscriptclosure)
    , and [`getscripthash`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/scripts/getscripthash)
     now support CoreScripts.
*   Added [`raknet.is_enabled(): boolean`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/raknet)
    .
*   Added [`setrbxclipboard(data: any)`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/miscellaneous/setrbxclipboard)
     for copying Studio-compatible data.
*   Added [`saveinstance`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/miscellaneous/saveinstance)
     and [`saveplace`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/miscellaneous/saveplace)
    .

[Internal UI](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#internal-ui)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

Volt 1.3 adds Game Explorer and Script Viewer to the internal UI. It also uses themes from the desktop interface for consistent styling.

Volt DarkCatppuccin MochaSakura Drift

![Volt internal UI using the Volt Dark theme](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/internal-ui-volt-dark.png)

![Volt internal UI using the Catppuccin Mocha theme](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/internal-ui-catppuccin-mocha.png)

![Volt internal UI using the Sakura Drift theme](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/internal-ui-sakura-drift.png)

### [Game Explorer](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#game-explorer)

Game Explorer shows the live game tree and a properties panel for viewing and editing the selected instances.

*   Hold `CTRL` to select multiple instances.
*   Use the instance context menu to copy, rename, or paste into an instance.
*   Use **Copy Path** to copy a Luau path for a property.
*   Use **Copy Name** to copy only the property name.
*   Use **Copy Value** to copy the value in Luau format.
*   Tags and attributes can be removed from their context menus.
*   **Save Place** displays progress while the place is being written.

### [Script Viewer](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#script-viewer)

Script Viewer displays decompiled script output with variable renaming, reference navigation, and bracket pair colorization.

*   Right-click a variable or press `X` to view its references.
*   Press `R` to rename the selected variable.

[Decompiler](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#decompiler)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

Volt 1.3 introduces a built-in Luau decompiler. [`decompile`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/scripts/decompile)
 accepts a script or raw Luau bytecode and returns source text.

*   [`DecompilerOptions`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/scripts/decompile#decompileroptions)
     controls variable naming, function declarations, guard clauses, conditional structuring, constant folding, scope block insertion, and formatting.
*   [`DecompilerFormatter`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/scripts/decompile#decompilerformatter)
     controls indentation, column limit, condition parentheses, semicolons, and function metadata.

The decompiler is still in development, so some bugs are expected. See the [Decompiler guide](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/decompiler)
 for usage details and current behavior.

[Save Instance](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#save-instance)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

[`saveinstance`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/miscellaneous/saveinstance)
 and [`saveplace`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/miscellaneous/saveplace)
 export instance hierarchies using the game's binary serialization format. They can run from scripts or from the internal UI.

Use [`SaveInstanceOptions`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/miscellaneous/saveinstance#saveinstanceoptions)
 to configure:

*   Output file path
*   Script decompilation
*   Clipboard output
*   An `IgnoreList` property (currently readable, but not assignable from Luau)
*   Decompiler options
*   Player and character filters
*   Nil instance filters
*   Non-creatable instance filters

[Desktop UI 1.0](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#desktop-ui-10)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

UI 1.0 introduces a redesigned desktop interface with a more compact layout, refreshed branding, updated app icons, and a cleaner default theme.

![Volt UI 1.0 home and setup screen](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/home.png)

Home and setup view

![Volt UI 1.0 editor screen](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/editor.png)

Editor view

### [Workspace](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#workspace)

*   Pinned tabs, with Home pinned by default, and dedicated pinned-tab controls.
*   Saved layouts that restore splits, open tabs, pane sizes, sidebar and terminal state, window placement, and unsaved tabs.
*   Detachable Terminals, Account Manager, and Theme Editor windows. Terminals include search and filtering, connection status, and topmost mode.
*   Instance groups, including an `All` group, with support for binding tabs to a group.
*   A searchable command palette for common actions.

### [Editor and files](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#editor-and-files)

*   Editor breadcrumbs with folder and file navigation.
*   Separate Scripts and Autoexec trees with search and folder creation.
*   Drag-and-drop file organization and support for opening external `.txt`, `.lua`, and `.luau` files.
*   Installed system font detection and font selection in Settings.
*   Dynamic source maps that keep Luau LSP completions and types synchronized with the live game tree.

### [Setup and settings](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#setup-and-settings)

*   One-click setup from Home, supported game version checks, and prompts to install the required version when needed.
*   Background Volt update checks and an API health check during startup.
*   Decompiler settings with live output previews and Save Instance default settings.
*   Theme schema v3 with expanded customization and theme import and export.
*   Windows 7 Aero, Volt Ware, and Voltmillion themes, with updated built-in Monaco styling.
*   A Theme Editor with collapsible sections and a responsive color picker, plus a keybind editor for the internal UI.

[Account Manager](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#account-manager)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Volt 1.3 introduces Account Manager as a public feature. It stores accounts, launches multiple clients, tracks running instances, and manages private server launches.

![Volt Account Manager launch settings and account table](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/account_manager_1.png)

Launch settings and account table

![Volt Account Manager compact performance overlay](https://docs.voltbz.net/img/overlay_1.png)

Performance overlay

It includes:

*   Browser login and bulk cookie import, with protected cookie storage and cookie health checks.
*   Multi-account launching with launch delays, stop controls, connection tracking, and Place ID validation.
*   Private server launching with none, round-robin, and assigned server modes.
*   Automatic relaunching for closed instances, with relaunch countdowns.
*   Per-account and total CPU and memory usage.
*   A configurable performance overlay showing account status, process count, CPU and memory usage, and relaunch state.
*   Memory Guard with automatic and fixed committed-memory limits.
*   Autosaved launch and private server settings.

[Related docs](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/releases/1.3#related-docs)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

# Volt Documentation

[Crypt](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt)

crypt.hash
==========

Generates a hash of the provided data.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#syntax)

---------------------------------------------------------

    crypt.hash(data: string, algorithm: string, key?: string) -> string

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#parameters)

-----------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `data` | `string` | The data to hash |
| `algorithm` | `string` | Required hash algorithm name |
| `key` | `string?` | Optional key for `BLAKE2B`; ignored by other algorithms |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#returns)

-----------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | The hexadecimal hash string |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#description)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

`crypt.hash` generates a cryptographic hash of the input data using the specified algorithm.

[Supported Algorithms](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#supported-algorithms)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `MD5`
*   `SHA1`
*   `SHA224`
*   `SHA256`
*   `SHA384`
*   `SHA512`
*   `SHA3-224`
*   `SHA3-256`
*   `SHA3-384`
*   `SHA3-512`
*   `BLAKE2B`

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#example)

-----------------------------------------------------------

    local data = "Hello, World!"
    
    local hash = crypt.hash(data, "SHA256")
    print("SHA256:", hash)
    
    -- Other algorithms
    print("MD5:", crypt.hash(data, "MD5"))
    print("SHA1:", crypt.hash(data, "SHA1"))
    print("SHA512:", crypt.hash(data, "SHA512"))

[Use Cases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#use-cases)

---------------------------------------------------------------

*   Verifying data integrity
*   Creating unique identifiers
*   Content fingerprints and integrity checks (not password storage)

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash#related-functions)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`crypt.hmac`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac)
     - HMAC authentication

# Volt Documentation

[Crypt](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt)

crypt.generatekey
=================

Generates a random encryption key.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey#syntax)

----------------------------------------------------------------

    crypt.generatekey() -> string

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey#returns)

------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | A Base64-encoded random key |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey#description)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

`crypt.generatekey` generates 32 random bytes and returns them as Base64, which is the key format accepted by `crypt.encrypt` and `crypt.decrypt`.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey#example)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Generate a key
    local key = crypt.generatekey()
    print("Generated key:", key)
    
    -- Use the key for encryption
    local data = "Secret message"
    local encrypted, iv = crypt.encrypt(data, key)
    local decrypted = crypt.decrypt(encrypted, key, iv, "CBC")
    
    print("Original:", data)
    print("Decrypted:", decrypted)

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey#notes)

--------------------------------------------------------------

*   The key is suitable for AES encryption
*   Keys should be stored securely and not shared

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatekey#related-functions)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`crypt.encrypt`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/encrypt)
     - Encrypt data
*   [`crypt.decrypt`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/decrypt)
     - Decrypt data
*   [`crypt.generatebytes`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/generatebytes)
     - Generate raw bytes

# Volt Documentation

[Crypt](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt)

crypt.hmac
==========

Generates an HMAC (Hash-based Message Authentication Code).

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#syntax)

---------------------------------------------------------

    crypt.hmac(key: string, data: string, algorithm: string) -> string

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#parameters)

-----------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `key` | `string` | The secret key |
| `data` | `string` | The data to authenticate |
| `algorithm` | `string` | Required hash algorithm name |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#returns)

-----------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | The HMAC as a Base64 string |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#description)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

`crypt.hmac` generates a keyed-hash message authentication code, which provides both data integrity and authentication.

[Supported Algorithms](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#supported-algorithms)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `MD5`
*   `SHA1`
*   `SHA224`
*   `SHA256`
*   `SHA384`
*   `SHA512`
*   `SHA3-224`, `SHA3-256`, `SHA3-384`, `SHA3-512`
*   `BLAKE2B`

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#example)

-----------------------------------------------------------

    local data = "Important message"
    local key = "secret_key"
    
    local mac = crypt.hmac(key, data, "SHA256")
    print("HMAC:", mac)

[Verification Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#verification-example)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function verifyMessage(data, key, expectedHmac)
        local computed = crypt.hmac(key, data, "SHA256")
        return computed == expectedHmac
    end
    
    local key = "my_secret"
    local message = "Hello"
    local signature = crypt.hmac(key, message, "SHA256")
    
    -- Later, verify the message
    if verifyMessage(message, key, signature) then
        print("Message is authentic!")
    end

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hmac#related-functions)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`crypt.hash`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/crypt/hash)
     - Simple hashing

[Console](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console)

rconsoleinfo
============

Prints an info message to the console.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinfo#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleinfo(data: any, async?: boolean) -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinfo#parameters)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `data` | `any` | Value to format after the `[INFO]` prefix |
| `async` | `boolean?` | Yield while writing on the console task (default: true) |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinfo#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinfo#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsoleinfo` prints an info-level message to the console, typically with a distinct color or prefix to indicate it's informational.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinfo#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow()
    
    rconsoleinfo("Script loaded successfully")
    rconsoleinfo("Found 5 players in game")

# Volt Documentation

[Console](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console)

rconsoleinput
=============

Gets input from the user via the console.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#syntax)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleinput() -> string

[Aliases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#aliases)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `consoleinput`

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#returns)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | The user's input |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#description)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`rconsoleinput` waits for the user to type something in the console and press Enter, then returns the input as a string. This is a yielding function.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#example)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow()
    
    rconsoleprint("What is your name? ")
    local name = rconsoleinput()
    
    rconsoleprint("Hello, " .. name .. "!\n")

[Interactive Menu Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#interactive-menu-example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    rconsoleshow()
    rconsolename("Script Menu")
    
    rconsoleprint("Select an option:\n")
    rconsoleprint("1. Option A\n")
    rconsoleprint("2. Option B\n")
    rconsoleprint("3. Exit\n\n")
    rconsoleprint("Enter choice: ")
    
    local choice = rconsoleinput()
    
    if choice == "1" then
        rconsoleprint("You selected Option A\n")
    elseif choice == "2" then
        rconsoleprint("You selected Option B\n")
    else
        rconsoleprint("Goodbye!\n")
        task.wait(1)
        rconsolehide()
    end

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#notes)

------------------------------------------------------------------

*   This function yields until the user presses Enter
*   Returns an empty string if the console is closed

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleinput#related-functions)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`rconsoleprint`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/console/rconsoleprint)
     - Print to console

# Volt Documentation

[Input](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input)

mousemoveabs
============

Moves the mouse cursor to an absolute screen position.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoveabs#syntax)

-----------------------------------------------------------------

    mousemoveabs(x: number, y: number) -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoveabs#parameters)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `x` | `number` | X coordinate in pixels |
| `y` | `number` | Y coordinate in pixels |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoveabs#returns)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoveabs#description)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

`mousemoveabs` moves the physical cursor to coordinates relative to the game client area. Volt ignores simulated input while the game window is not focused.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoveabs#example)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Move to top-left corner
    mousemoveabs(0, 0)
    
    -- Move to center of screen (assuming 1920x1080)
    mousemoveabs(960, 540)
    
    -- Move to specific button location
    mousemoveabs(500, 300)
    mouse1click()

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoveabs#related-functions)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`mousemoverel`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel)
     - Move relative to current position

# Volt Documentation

[Input](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input)

mousemoverel
============

Moves the mouse cursor relative to its current position.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#syntax)

-----------------------------------------------------------------

    mousemoverel(dx: number, dy: number) -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#parameters)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `dx` | `number` | Horizontal offset in pixels |
| `dy` | `number` | Vertical offset in pixels |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#returns)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#description)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

`mousemoverel` moves the physical cursor relative to its current position. Volt ignores simulated input while the game window is not focused.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#example)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Move right 100 pixels
    mousemoverel(100, 0)
    
    -- Move down 50 pixels
    mousemoverel(0, 50)
    
    -- Move diagonally
    mousemoverel(50, 50)

[Circle Motion](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#circle-motion)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Draw a circle with mouse movement
    for i = 0, 360, 5 do
        local rad = math.rad(i)
        local dx = math.cos(rad) * 2
        local dy = math.sin(rad) * 2
        mousemoverel(dx, dy)
        task.wait(0.01)
    end

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/input/mousemoverel#related-functions)

     - Move to absolute position

# Volt Documentation

[Closures](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures)

getfunctionhash
===============

Returns a hash of the function's bytecode.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/getfunctionhash#syntax)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

    getfunctionhash(func: function) -> string

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/getfunctionhash#parameters)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `func` | `function` | The function to hash |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/getfunctionhash#returns)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | A 96-character hexadecimal SHA-384 digest |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/getfunctionhash#description)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`getfunctionhash` computes a SHA-384 digest over a Luau closure's instructions and constants. It throws for C closures, which have no Luau bytecode to hash.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/getfunctionhash#example)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function isSha384Hex(hash)
        return #hash == 96 and hash:match("^[0-9a-fA-F]+$") ~= nil
    end
    
    local function first()
        return "constant"
    end
    
    local function second()
        return "different constant"
    end
    
    local firstHash = getfunctionhash(first)
    local secondHash = getfunctionhash(second)
    
    print(isSha384Hex(firstHash)) -- true
    print(firstHash == secondHash) -- false

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/getfunctionhash#notes)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Only works with Luau closures; C closures throw an error
*   Slight changes in function bytecode will alter the returned hash

# Volt Documentation

[Closures](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures)

checkcaller
===========

Returns whether the current function was called by Volt.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/checkcaller#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    checkcaller() -> boolean

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/checkcaller#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `boolean` | `true` if called from a Volt thread, `false` otherwise |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/checkcaller#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`checkcaller` returns a boolean indicating whether the current function was invoked from Volt's own thread. This is useful for differentiating between your own calls and those made by the game.

When you hook a game function, both your code and the game will trigger it. Use `checkcaller` to determine whether to run custom logic or pass through to the original.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures/checkcaller#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    local old
    old = hookfunction(game.HttpGet, function(self, url, ...)
        if checkcaller() then
            -- Call is from our script, allow it
            return old(self, url, ...)
        end
    
        -- Call is from the game, we can block or modify it
        print("Game tried to fetch:", url)
        return old(self, url, ...)
    end)
    
    -- This will pass checkcaller() because we're calling it
    local result = game:HttpGet("https://example.com")

\
Previous Page](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/closures)

[Environment](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment)

getrenv
=======

Returns the game's global environment table.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#syntax)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    getrenv() -> table

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#returns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `table` | The game's global environment |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#description)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`getrenv` returns the global environment used by game scripts. This contains standard globals such as `game`, `workspace`, and `Instance`.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    local renv = getrenv()
    
    -- Access game globals
    print(renv.game)      -- Same as game
    print(renv.workspace) -- Same as workspace
    print(renv.Instance)  -- Same as Instance

[Finding Game Globals](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#finding-game-globals)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- List all globals in the game environment
    local renv = getrenv()
    for name, value in pairs(renv) do
        print(name, type(value))
    end

[Difference from getgenv](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#difference-from-getgenv)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| `getrenv()` | `getgenv()` |
| --- | --- |
| Game's environment | Volt's environment |
| Contains game globals | Contains Volt globals |
| Read-only access recommended | Can freely modify |

[Checking for Global Modifications](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getrenv#checking-for-global-modifications)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Check if game modified a global
    local renv = getrenv()
    if renv.print ~= print then
        warn("print function was modified!")
    end

# Volt Documentation

[Environment](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment)

getgenv
=======

Returns Volt's global environment table.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#syntax)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    getgenv() -> table

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#returns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `table` | Volt's global environment |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#description)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`getgenv` returns the global environment table used by Volt. Variables stored here persist across different script executions and can be accessed by all scripts running in Volt.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Store a value in the Volt environment
    getgenv().myGlobalValue = "Hello from script 1"
    
    -- Later, in another script execution
    print(getgenv().myGlobalValue) -- "Hello from script 1"

[Shared State](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#shared-state)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Script 1: Initialize shared state
    getgenv().SharedData = {
        players = {},
        settings = {
            enabled = true
        }
    }
    
    -- Script 2: Access shared state
    if getgenv().SharedData then
        print("Settings:", getgenv().SharedData.settings.enabled)
    end

[Checking if Script Already Ran](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#checking-if-script-already-ran)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    if getgenv().MyScriptLoaded then
        warn("Script already running!")
        return
    end
    getgenv().MyScriptLoaded = true
    
    -- Rest of your script...

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/environment/getgenv#related-functions)

# Volt Documentation

[WebSocket](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket)

WebSocket.OnMessage
===================

Signal that fires when a message is received from the server.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#syntax)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    WebSocket.OnMessage:Connect(callback: (message: string, isBinary: boolean) -> ()) -> VoltConnection

[Callback Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#callback-parameters)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `message` | `string` | The received message |
| `isBinary` | `boolean` | Whether the message arrived as a binary frame |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#returns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `VoltConnection` | A connection that can be disconnected |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#description)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`OnMessage` is a `VoltSignal` that fires for each message frame received from the server. Text and binary payloads are both returned as Luau strings; use `isBinary` to distinguish the frame type.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    ws.OnMessage:Connect(function(message, isBinary)
        print("Received:", message, "binary:", isBinary)
    end)
    
    ws:Send("Hello!")

[JSON Messages](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#json-messages)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local HttpService = game:GetService("HttpService")
    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    ws.OnMessage:Connect(function(message, isBinary)
        if isBinary then
            return
        end
    
        local data = HttpService:JSONDecode(message)
        
        if data.type == "update" then
            print("Update:", data.payload)
        elseif data.type == "error" then
            warn("Error:", data.message)
        end
    end)

[Message Queue Pattern](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#message-queue-pattern)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    local messageQueue = {}
    
    ws.OnMessage:Connect(function(message, isBinary)
        table.insert(messageQueue, {
            content = message,
            binary = isBinary,
            time = os.time()
        })
    end)
    
    -- Process messages elsewhere
    local function processQueue()
        for _, msg in ipairs(messageQueue) do
            print(msg.time, msg.content, "binary:", msg.binary)
        end
        table.clear(messageQueue)
    end

[Related](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage#related)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`WebSocket.connect`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect)
     - Create connection
*   [`WebSocket:Send`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/send)
     - Send messages
*   [`OnClose`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose)
     - Connection close event

# Volt Documentation

[WebSocket](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket)

WebSocket.OnClose
=================

Signal that fires when the WebSocket connection is closed.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#syntax)

----------------------------------------------------------------

    WebSocket.OnClose:Connect(callback: () -> ()) -> VoltConnection

[Callback Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#callback-parameters)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This callback receives no parameters.

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#returns)

------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `VoltConnection` | A connection that can be disconnected |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#description)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

`OnClose` is a `VoltSignal` that fires after a local `Close()` or a remote close. Volt may reconnect automatically after a remote close; `IsClosed` becomes true only when the client is closed locally.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#example)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    ws.OnClose:Connect(function()
        print("Connection closed")
    end)
    
    -- Later...
    ws:Close()

[Reconnection Pattern](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#reconnection-pattern)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function createConnection()
        local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
        
        ws.OnClose:Connect(function()
            print("Disconnected, reconnecting in 5 seconds...")
            task.wait(5)
            createConnection()
        end)
        
        ws.OnMessage:Connect(function(msg)
            print("Message:", msg)
        end)
        
        return ws
    end
    
    local connection = createConnection()

[Cleanup on Close](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#cleanup-on-close)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    ws.OnClose:Connect(function()
        print("Cleaning up...")
        -- Perform cleanup tasks
    end)
    
    -- Check connection state before sending
    if not ws.IsClosed then
        ws:Send("Hello!")
    end

[Related](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose#related)

------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`WebSocket.connect`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect)
     - Create connection
*   [`WebSocket:Close`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close)
     - Close connection
*   [`OnMessage`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onmessage)
     - Message event

# Volt Documentation

[WebSocket](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket)

WebSocket:Close
===============

Closes the WebSocket connection.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#syntax)

--------------------------------------------------------------

    WebSocket:Close() -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#parameters)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

This method takes no parameters.

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#returns)

----------------------------------------------------------------

This method does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#description)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

`WebSocket:Close` closes the connection. `IsClosed` becomes true and `OnClose` fires when closing completes. Later `Send` and `Close` calls have no effect.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#example)

----------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    ws.OnClose:Connect(function()
        print("Connection closed")
    end)
    
    -- Use the connection
    ws:Send("Hello!")
    
    -- Close after 10 seconds
    task.wait(10)
    ws:Close()

[Cleanup Pattern](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#cleanup-pattern)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    local function cleanup()
        if ws then
            ws:Close()
            ws = nil
        end
    end
    
    -- Close on script end or when done
    cleanup()

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#notes)

------------------------------------------------------------

*   A locally closed client cannot be reopened
*   `Close` is safe to call multiple times
*   Use `IsClosed` to inspect the local closed state

[Related](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/close#related)

----------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`WebSocket.connect`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect)
     - Create connection
*   [`OnClose`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/onclose)
     - Close event

# Volt Documentation

[WebSocket](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket)

WebSocket.connect
=================

Creates a new WebSocket connection to the specified URL.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#syntax)

----------------------------------------------------------------

    WebSocket.connect(url: string) -> WebSocket

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#parameters)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `url` | `string` | The WebSocket URL to connect to |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#returns)

------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `WebSocket` | A WebSocket object with methods and events |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#description)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

`WebSocket.connect` yields while Volt establishes a connection. It returns a WebSocket object after a successful handshake and raises an error if the connection fails.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#example)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Replace this with a WebSocket endpoint you control
    local ws = WebSocket.connect("wss://your-server.example/socket")
    
    -- Handle incoming messages
    ws.OnMessage:Connect(function(message, isBinary)
        print("Received:", message, "binary:", isBinary)
    end)
    
    -- Handle connection close
    ws.OnClose:Connect(function()
        print("Connection closed")
    end)
    
    -- Send a message
    ws:Send("Hello, WebSocket!")

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#notes)

--------------------------------------------------------------

*   Use a `ws://` or `wss://` WebSocket URL; `wss://` encrypts the connection
*   Volt attempts to reconnect automatically after a remote disconnection
*   Call `Close` to stop the client intentionally

[Related](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/websocket/connect#related)

------------------------------------------------------------------

# Volt Documentation

[Reflection](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection)

isscriptable
============

Checks if a property is scriptable.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#syntax)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

    isscriptable(object: Object, property: string) -> boolean

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#parameters)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `object` | `Object` | The object, including an `Instance` |
| `property` | `string` | The property name |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#returns)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `boolean` | Whether the property is currently scriptable |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#description)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`isscriptable` checks whether a property is scriptable. An invalid property name raises an error.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#example)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local part = Instance.new("Part")
    
    -- Check common properties
    print(isscriptable(part, "Name"))     -- true (visible)
    print(isscriptable(part, "Position")) -- true (visible)
    print(isscriptable(part, "size_xml")) -- false (hidden)

[Use Case](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#use-case)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function getAllProperties(instance)
        local properties = {}
        
        -- Check a list of known property names
        local propertyNames = {"Name", "Position", "Size", "Color", "size_xml"}
        
        for _, propName in ipairs(propertyNames) do
            local scriptable = isscriptable(instance, propName)
            properties[propName] = {
                scriptable = scriptable,
                value = scriptable and instance[propName] or gethiddenproperty(instance, propName)
            }
        end
        
        return properties
    end

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable#related-functions)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`setscriptable`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable)
     - Change scriptability
*   [`gethiddenproperty`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/gethiddenproperty)
     - Get hidden property values

# Volt Documentation

[Reflection](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection)

setscriptable
=============

Sets whether a property is scriptable.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#syntax)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

    setscriptable(object: Object, property: string, scriptable: boolean) -> boolean

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#parameters)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `object` | `Object` | The object, including an `Instance` |
| `property` | `string` | The property name |
| `scriptable` | `boolean` | The new scriptability |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#returns)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `boolean` | The previous scriptability; false is returned for a property that cannot be resolved |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#description)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`setscriptable` changes whether a property is accessible through normal scripting. This can make hidden properties accessible via regular property access.

Not every hidden property supports this operation. Changing scriptability can also be observable by game code, so restore the previous value as soon as it is no longer needed.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#example)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local part = Instance.new("Part")
    
    -- Make a hidden property scriptable
    local wasScriptable = setscriptable(part, "size_xml", true)
    
    -- Now we can access it normally
    print(part.size_xml)
    
    -- Restore original state
    setscriptable(part, "size_xml", wasScriptable)

[Making Hidden Properties Accessible](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#making-hidden-properties-accessible)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function exposeProperty(instance, propName)
        local wasScriptable = setscriptable(instance, propName, true)
        return function()
            setscriptable(instance, propName, wasScriptable)
        end
    end
    
    -- Use
    local restore = exposeProperty(part, "size_xml")
    print(part.size_xml) -- Now accessible
    restore() -- Restore original state

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/setscriptable#related-functions)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`isscriptable`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/isscriptable)
     - Check scriptability
*   [`gethiddenproperty`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/reflection/gethiddenproperty)
     - Alternative for hidden properties

[Filesystem](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem)

listfiles
=========

Lists all files and folders in a directory.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#syntax)

-------------------------------------------------------------------

    listfiles(path: string) -> table

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#parameters)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path` | `string` | The folder path |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#returns)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `table` | Array of file and folder paths |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#description)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`listfiles` returns a table containing the paths of all files and folders within the specified directory.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#example)

---------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Create some files and folders
    makefolder("mydata")
    writefile("mydata/file1.txt", "Hello")
    writefile("mydata/file2.txt", "World")
    makefolder("mydata/subfolder")
    
    -- List contents
    local files = listfiles("mydata")
    for _, path in ipairs(files) do
        print(path)
    end
    --[[\
    mydata/file1.txt\
    mydata/file2.txt\
    mydata/subfolder\
    ]]

[Filtering Files and Folders](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#filtering-files-and-folders)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function getFiles(path)
        local files = {}
        for _, item in ipairs(listfiles(path)) do
            if isfile(item) then
                table.insert(files, item)
            end
        end
        return files
    end
    
    local function getFolders(path)
        local folders = {}
        for _, item in ipairs(listfiles(path)) do
            if isfolder(item) then
                table.insert(folders, item)
            end
        end
        return folders
    end

[Recursive Listing](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#recursive-listing)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function listAllFiles(path, results)
        results = results or {}
        for _, item in ipairs(listfiles(path)) do
            if isfile(item) then
                table.insert(results, item)
            elseif isfolder(item) then
                listAllFiles(item, results)
            end
        end
        return results
    end
    
    local allFiles = listAllFiles("mydata")

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/listfiles#related-functions)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

     - Check if path is a file
*   [`isfolder`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/isfolder)
     - Check if path is a folder

makefolder
==========

Creates a new folder.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#syntax)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    makefolder(path: string) -> ()

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#parameters)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path` | `string` | The folder path to create |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#returns)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return a value.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#description)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`makefolder` creates a new folder at the specified path. Parent folders are created automatically if they don't exist.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#example)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Create a simple folder
    makefolder("configs")
    
    -- Create nested folders
    makefolder("data/saves/player1")
    
    -- Verify they exist
    print(isfolder("configs"))           -- true
    print(isfolder("data/saves/player1")) -- true

[Ensure Folder Exists](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#ensure-folder-exists)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function ensureFolder(path)
        if not isfolder(path) then
            makefolder(path)
            return true -- Created
        end
        return false -- Already existed
    end
    
    if ensureFolder("logs") then
        print("Created logs folder")
    end

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/makefolder#related-functions)

# Volt Documentation

[Filesystem](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem)

dofile
======

Loads and executes a Luau file.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#syntax)

----------------------------------------------------------------

    dofile(path: string) -> ()

[Aliases](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#aliases)

------------------------------------------------------------------

*   `runfile`

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#parameters)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path` | `string` | Path to the Luau file |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#returns)

------------------------------------------------------------------

This function does not return values from the executed file.

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#description)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

`dofile` reads a Luau file from the workspace folder, compiles it, and executes it. Compilation and runtime errors propagate to the caller. Values returned by the file are discarded.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#example)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Create a script file
    writefile("greet.lua", [[\
        print("Hello from greet.lua!")\
    ]])
    
    -- Compile and execute it
    dofile("greet.lua")

[Execute Script](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#execute-script)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Create and run a script
    writefile("script.lua", [[\
        print("Script executed!")\
        for i = 1, 5 do\
            print("Count:", i)\
        end\
    ]])
    
    dofile("script.lua")

[Notes](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#notes)

--------------------------------------------------------------

*   This is a yielding function
*   File must contain valid Luau code
*   Return values from the file are discarded; use `loadfile` when they are needed
*   Errors in the file will propagate

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/dofile#related-functions)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`loadfile`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/loadfile)
     - Load without executing
*   [`readfile`](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile)
     - Read file as string

[Filesystem](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem)

readfile
========

Reads the contents of a file.

[Syntax](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#syntax)

------------------------------------------------------------------

    readfile(path: string) -> string

[Parameters](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#parameters)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Parameter | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path` | `string` | The file path |

[Returns](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#returns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string` | The file contents |

[Description](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#description)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

`readfile` reads and returns the entire contents of a file as a string.

[Example](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#example)

--------------------------------------------------------------------

    -- Write and then read a file
    writefile("message.txt", "Hello, World!")
    local content = readfile("message.txt")
    print(content) -- "Hello, World!"

[Reading JSON](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#reading-json)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local HttpService = game:GetService("HttpService")
    
    -- Write JSON data
    local data = {name = "Player", score = 100}
    writefile("save.json", HttpService:JSONEncode(data))
    
    -- Read and parse JSON
    local content = readfile("save.json")
    local loaded = HttpService:JSONDecode(content)
    print(loaded.name, loaded.score) -- "Player" 100

[Error Handling](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#error-handling)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    local function safeRead(path)
        local success, result = pcall(readfile, path)
        if success then
            return result
        else
            warn("Failed to read:", result)
            return nil
        end
    end

[Related Functions](https://docs.voltbz.net/docs/filesystem/readfile#related-functions)
